### The Future of Diversified Manufacturing in Southwest Virginia

#### The Role of VCEDA in Supporting Manufacturing Diversification

The Virginia Coalfield Economic Development Authority has played a central role in supporting manufacturing diversification in the region, providing loans, grants, and workforce development support that enable companies to expand and create jobs . The Authority's support for manufacturing reflects its recognition that the sector provides stable, well-paying jobs that are essential to the region's economic future . VCEDA's investment in manufacturing has been substantial, with loans and grants supporting expansions, equipment purchases, and workforce training .

The effectiveness of VCEDA's approach is demonstrated by the success of the companies it has supported. The Tate expansion, which was supported by a $4 million loan, has created 35 new jobs in Russell County . The VFP expansion, supported by a $312,500 grant and a $3 million loan, has created 200 new jobs in Scott County . The West River Conveyors expansion, supported by a $4 million loan, has preserved manufacturing capacity and created jobs in Buchanan County . The White Rock Truss expansion, supported by a $500,000 loan, has created 27 new jobs in Lee County .

The diversity of the manufacturing sector is a strength that has enabled the region to weather the decline of coal. Companies like Tate and VFP serve the data center and utility industries, while West River Conveyors serves the mining and materials handling industries, and White Rock Truss serves the construction industry . This diversity provides a buffer against downturns in any single industry, ensuring that the manufacturing sector can continue to provide employment and economic activity even when one industry is facing challenges .

#### Workforce Development and Talent Pipeline

The success of the manufacturing sector depends on the availability of a skilled workforce, and the region has made significant investments in workforce development and training . The VCEDA workforce development and training grants provided to VFP and other manufacturers support the training of new employees, ensuring that workers have the skills needed to operate advanced manufacturing equipment . The Virginia Talent Accelerator Program, which provides recruitment and training services at no cost to eligible companies, supports the development of the workforce needed for manufacturing growth .

The Workforce Housing Economic Development Incentive Pilot Program, which provided a $1 million award for the VFP expansion, addresses the housing challenges that can constrain economic growth . The program will create at least 50 new housing units reserved for households earning between 80 and 150 percent of the Area Median Income, ensuring that workers have access to affordable housing . This innovative approach to workforce development recognizes that housing availability is essential to attracting and retaining workers .

The region's community colleges and training providers play a critical role in workforce development, providing the training needed for manufacturing jobs . The partnership between companies, training providers, and economic development organizations ensures that the training provided is relevant and effective, meeting the needs of both the companies and the workers . The career pathways created by these training programs enable workers to advance from entry-level positions to higher-skilled roles, contributing to their economic well-being and the success of the manufacturing sector .

#### Transportation and Infrastructure

The manufacturing sector depends on reliable transportation infrastructure, and the region has made significant investments in roads, highways, and industrial parks . The West River Conveyors relocation to Southern Gap, which is within 1,000 feet of the Route 744 intersection of the new Coalfields Expressway, demonstrates the importance of transportation access to manufacturing operations . The Coalfields Expressway will provide improved transportation access to the region, supporting the growth of the manufacturing sector .

The former Alcoa building in Lebanon, where Tate is expanding, is located adjacent to four-lane U.S. Route 19, providing excellent transportation access for the company's operations . The facility's 13 docks and overhead doors support efficient logistics operations, enabling the company to receive raw materials and ship finished products efficiently . The availability of suitable industrial sites with transportation access is essential to the growth of the manufacturing sector .

The Southern Gap Industrial Park, where West River Conveyors is relocating, represents an investment in industrial infrastructure that supports manufacturing growth . The park provides sites with utilities, roads, and other infrastructure needed for manufacturing operations . The availability of such sites is essential to attracting and retaining manufacturing companies in the region .

#### The Future of Manufacturing in the Region

The future of manufacturing in the Southwest Virginia region is bright, with the expansions of Tate, VFP, West River Conveyors, and White Rock Truss demonstrating the potential for the sector to grow and diversify . The investments in manufacturing are creating jobs, generating economic activity, and building a stronger industrial base for the region . The success of these companies provides a model for other manufacturers in the region, demonstrating that manufacturing can thrive in Southwest Virginia .

The continued support of VCEDA and other economic development organizations will be essential to the growth of the manufacturing sector . The loans, grants, and workforce development programs that these organizations provide enable companies to invest in expansion and modernization . The partnership between the public and private sectors is essential to the success of the manufacturing sector, with the public sector providing the financing and infrastructure that enable private companies to invest and grow .

The manufacturing sector will be a pillar of the region's diversified economy, providing stable, well-paying jobs for workers in the region . The success of companies like Tate, VFP, West River Conveyors, and White Rock Truss demonstrates the potential for manufacturing to thrive in Southwest Virginia, even as the coal industry continues its long-term decline . The future of manufacturing in the region is bright, and its growth is central to the broader economic revitalization of the Southwest Virginia region .

# Chapter Nine: Technology Integration for Diversified Manufacturing

## Section 9.3: The lowL Advantage for Manufacturing Companies

### The Strategic Imperative for Modern Manufacturing Software

The manufacturing companies operating in the Tazewell County region—Tate, VFP, Inc., West River Conveyors, and White Rock Truss—operate in increasingly competitive markets where efficiency, precision, and adaptability determine success. The integration of modern software platforms, real-time control systems, and robotics represents a significant opportunity to enhance productivity, reduce costs, and improve product quality. The lowL compiler and its associated operating systems provide a foundation for this technological transformation, enabling companies to develop control systems that are both efficient and reliable [citation:compiler].

The manufacturing sector has historically relied on programmable logic controllers (PLCs) programmed in low-level languages such as Instruction List (IL), which is an assembly-like language close to the hardware . While these languages provide the control needed for industrial applications, they require specialized skills and extensive manual effort. The lowL compiler provides a modern alternative that combines the control of low-level languages with the readability and productivity of higher-level languages, enabling companies to develop control systems more efficiently and effectively [citation:compiler].

The transition from traditional PLC programming to lowL-based control systems offers significant advantages for the region's manufacturers. The compiler's support for inline assembly, hardware builtins, and SIMD vector types enables developers to write code that is optimized for specific hardware platforms [citation:compiler]. The compiler's optimization levels, ranging from O0 to O3, provide the flexibility to balance code size and performance [citation:compiler]. The compiler's data sections feature enables efficient management of configuration data and motion profiles [citation:compiler]. These features combine to create a development environment that is both powerful and productive.

### The lowL Compiler Architecture and Capabilities

The lowL compiler, implemented in Python with version 2.1.0, provides a comprehensive set of features for systems programming that are directly applicable to manufacturing control systems [citation:compiler]. The compiler's support for x86_64 architectures makes it compatible with the embedded processors used in many manufacturing applications, while its features enable developers to write code that is optimized for specific requirements [citation:compiler]. The compiler's modular architecture enables customization and extension, ensuring that it can adapt to the evolving needs of the manufacturing industry.

The compiler's inline assembly feature (the `asm` statement) enables developers to incorporate assembly code directly into their high-level lowL programs, providing the control needed for real-time hardware interaction [citation:compiler]. This is essential for manufacturing control systems, where precise timing and direct hardware access are often required. The compiler's hardware builtins, including `cli`, `sti`, `hlt`, `pause`, `rdtsc`, and `cpuid`, provide access to the processor's capabilities, enabling developers to write code that is optimized for the specific hardware platform [citation:compiler].

The compiler's SIMD vector types, including `vec4_f32`, `vec8_f32`, and `vec16_f32`, enable parallel processing that can significantly accelerate computationally intensive tasks such as sensor fusion, real-time control, and signal processing [citation:compiler]. For manufacturing applications, this acceleration can enable higher control loop frequencies, more precise motion control, and more sophisticated quality monitoring. The compiler's BlockArray data structure, which is SIMD-optimized, provides a high-performance dynamic array that is suitable for real-time applications [citation:compiler].

The compiler's support for pattern matching switch statements, with `when` guards and priority, provides a powerful mechanism for handling complex control logic [citation:compiler]. This enables developers to write code that is both readable and efficient, reducing the risk of errors and improving maintainability. The compiler's `#[interrupt]` and `#[kernel]` attributes provide the capability to develop interrupt handlers and kernel modules, essential for real-time control systems that must respond quickly to events [citation:compiler].

### AlgorithmicOS: A Real-Time Operating System for Manufacturing

AlgorithmicOS, the operating system for robots written entirely in lowL, provides a framework for developing real-time control systems that can be adapted to the specific requirements of different manufacturing applications [citation:robotics_book]. The operating system provides a cooperative round-robin scheduler that enables deterministic, low-latency task management [citation:robotics_book]. This deterministic scheduling is essential for manufacturing applications, where precise timing and predictable performance are critical.

The operating system's wireless stack, which implements a lightweight UDP/IP protocol over Wi-Fi or BLE, enables communication between control systems and external devices [citation:robotics_book]. This communication capability is essential for modern manufacturing systems, which often require coordination between multiple machines, sensors, and control systems. The operating system's command parser, which uses pattern matching to dispatch incoming commands, provides a flexible and efficient mechanism for handling external inputs [citation:robotics_book].

The operating system's actuator manager provides real-time control of servos and motors, with support for interpolation and motion profiles [citation:robotics_book]. This capability is directly applicable to manufacturing applications, where precise control of actuators is essential for product quality and consistency. The operating system's safety monitor, which checks for watchdog timeouts, power conditions, and physical limits, provides the comprehensive safety monitoring required for industrial applications [citation:robotics_book].

The operating system's power manager provides sleep modes, battery monitoring, and charge control, enabling efficient power management in embedded manufacturing systems [citation:robotics_book]. The operating system's update manager provides over-the-air firmware updates, enabling manufacturers to deploy improvements and fixes without physically accessing the equipment [citation:robotics_book]. This capability is particularly valuable for manufacturers with equipment deployed in the field, as it reduces maintenance costs and enables continuous improvement.

### Application to Tate: Data Center Components Manufacturing

Tate's manufacturing operations in Russell County, which produce raised access floors, modular aisle containment, structural ceilings, and liquid-cooling technologies for data centers, can benefit significantly from the integration of lowL-based control systems and robotics [citation:tate]. The manufacturing of data center components requires precision in cutting, forming, and assembly, processes that are well-suited to automation and robotic control. The integration of lowL-based control systems would enable Tate to enhance the precision, consistency, and efficiency of its manufacturing operations.

The fabrication of raised access floor panels, for example, requires precise cutting, forming, and assembly to ensure that the panels meet the dimensional tolerances required for data center installations. lowL-based control systems could provide the real-time control needed for automated cutting and forming equipment, with the SIMD acceleration enabling high-speed processing of sensor data for quality monitoring. The compiler's support for interrupt handling would enable immediate response to equipment faults, reducing downtime and improving productivity.

The assembly of modular aisle containment systems requires precise positioning of components and reliable fastening operations. Robotic systems controlled by lowL-based software could automate the assembly process, with the robots guided by machine vision systems that ensure accurate positioning. The lowL compiler's support for SIMD operations would accelerate the image processing required for machine vision, enabling real-time inspection and quality control. The integration of robotics would also enable Tate to handle the growing demand for its products more efficiently, increasing production capacity without expanding the workforce proportionally.

The automation of Tate's manufacturing operations would also enhance safety, reducing the exposure of workers to hazardous processes and heavy materials. The safety monitoring capabilities of AlgorithmicOS, which include watchdog timers, power monitoring, and physical limit checking, would provide the comprehensive safety protection required for industrial automation. The system's ability to respond to emergency conditions through high-priority interrupts would ensure that safety is maintained at all times.

The compiler's support for interrupt handling would enable immediate response to equipment faults, reducing downtime and improving productivity.

The robotic welding systems, controlled by lowL-based software, could automate the welding of conveyor components, ensuring consistent weld quality and reducing the need for manual inspection. ### The RoboBallet Revolution: AI-Coordinated Multi-Robot Systems

The RoboBallet system, developed by scientists at UCL, Google DeepMind, and Intrinsic, represents a transformative advancement in multi-robot coordination, enabling teams of robotic arms to work together efficiently and safely in shared industrial environments . The system uses a graph neural network trained with reinforcement learning to enable robots to understand and reason about their surroundings, working out the most effective way to collaborate . RoboBallet can generate high-quality plans in just seconds, even for complex layouts it has never seen before, solving up to 40 tasks with eight robotic arms .

The application of RoboBallet to manufacturing operations in the Tazewell County region would enable a new level of flexibility and efficiency. The system's ability to coordinate multiple robotic arms would be particularly valuable for complex assembly operations, where multiple robots must work together without colliding. The RoboBallet researchers note that the system "could allow factories to adapt instantly if a robot fails or if the layout changes," providing the flexibility needed for modern manufacturing .

The RoboBallet system's scalability is a major breakthrough, as traditional planning algorithms struggle to handle more than a few robots due to the exponential growth in complexity . RoboBallet's graph-based architecture allows it to learn general principles of coordination, making it suitable for large-scale industrial use . The system's ability to handle up to eight robots and 40 tasks simultaneously would be directly applicable to the manufacturing operations of companies in the region.

The integration of RoboBallet with lowL-based control systems would provide a comprehensive automation solution, with the AI system providing high-level coordination and the lowL-based systems providing real-time control. The Graph Neural Network architecture that powers RoboBallet could be trained on data from the region's manufacturing operations, enabling the system to optimize task allocation, scheduling, and motion planning for specific manufacturing environments .

### Intrinsic's Software-First Workcell Automation

Intrinsic, a company under Alphabet, has developed a modular, software-first robotic workcell design powered by IntrinsicOS that is transforming industrial automation . The platform aims to democratize industrial AI by replacing complex robot coding with drag-and-drop automation, making robotic automation more accessible to manufacturers . The workcell runs on IntrinsicOS, leveraging AI to quickly implement skills-based automation for complex assembly .

Some of the skills enabled by Intrinsic's platform include instant process and tool reconfigurations, enabling "high mix" production for smaller custom batches . This capability is particularly valuable for manufacturers like VFP and West River Conveyors, which produce custom products for diverse customers. The ability to reconfigure processes quickly would enable these companies to respond more rapidly to customer requirements, reducing lead times and improving customer satisfaction.

Intrinsic is working with CNC system integrators such as Trinity Automation and MartinSystems to help these companies integrate AI skills and capabilities seamlessly into their next-generation products . The systems are built for easy, manageable use on the machine shop floor, without the need to program a robot anymore . The integration of AI capabilities, such as perception, automated robot motion planning, and the ability to grasp and insert parts, brings robotic assistance directly to machine operators .

The integration of Intrinsic's platform with lowL-based control systems would provide a comprehensive automation solution for the region's manufacturers. The lowL-based systems would provide the real-time control needed for precise operations, while Intrinsic's platform would provide the high-level automation and AI capabilities. The combination would enable manufacturers to deploy automation more quickly and effectively, without the need for extensive robotics programming expertise.

### Stanford's Multi-Robot Assembly Planning Algorithm

Researchers at Stanford University have developed an algorithm that can take a design plan for a particular product and figure out the most efficient way to manufacture it with a team of robots . The algorithm determines how the robots will split up to construct subassemblies and how to bring these pieces together quickly and efficiently . The researchers note that their objective is "to go from raw material to the finished product as quickly as possible" through parallelization .

The algorithm lays out assembly stations and assigns specific robots to collect and deliver parts to the correct stations at the correct times . It directs the robots to work in teams when parts are too large for an individual robot to carry and maps out how the robots will move to avoid interfering with others . The algorithm is remarkably fast, generating plans for a 1,845-part assembly in less than three minutes .

The application of this algorithm to the manufacturing operations of companies in the Tazewell County region would enable a new level of efficiency. The ability to generate assembly plans quickly would enable manufacturers to reconfigure their operations rapidly, responding to changes in product demand or design. The algorithm's support for parallelization would maximize throughput, enabling manufacturers to produce more products in less time.

The Stanford algorithm could be integrated with lowL-based control systems to provide a comprehensive automation solution. The algorithm would provide the high-level assembly plan, while the lowL-based systems would execute the real-time control of the robots. The combination would enable manufacturers to deploy automated assembly systems quickly and effectively, without the need for extensive manual planning.

### LLM-Powered Low-Code Programming for Industrial Robotics

A next-generation low-code programming framework empowered by large language models (LLMs) has been developed to advance human-centric smart manufacturing . The framework integrates the reasoning capabilities of LLMs into industrial robotic systems, prioritizing intuitive, efficient, and operator-friendly interaction . The system incorporates a cognitive assistance module to reduce the cognitive burden on unskilled operators .

An LLM-based low-code programming module was designed, employing a multi-agent mechanism for intent recognition, parameter extraction, and human verification . This significantly enhances the system's ability to robustly handle unstructured natural language instructions in industrial environments . The system was validated through a case study on aircraft panel drilling, demonstrating its practicality and reliability while supporting unskilled operators in performing complex tasks .

The application of this technology to manufacturing operations in the Tazewell County region would enable a new level of accessibility for automation. Operators could use natural language to program robots and control systems, reducing the need for specialized programming skills. The system's cognitive assistance would enable unskilled operators to perform complex tasks, reducing the need for extensive training.

The integration of LLM-powered programming with lowL-based control systems would provide a comprehensive automation solution. The LLM system would enable intuitive programming and control, while the lowL-based systems would provide the real-time performance and reliability needed for manufacturing applications. The combination would enable manufacturers to deploy automation more widely and effectively.

### The Güdel Automated Material Handling System

The ZP-6 overhead gantry with a two-axis robot from Güdel, implemented in the Simmons Machine Tool Corporation Wheel Turning Center, provides a compelling example of how automation can transform manufacturing operations . The system automates the process of moving heavy railway wheels into and out of machining centers, eliminating the need for manual handling with forklifts or cranes . The system handles wheels weighing up to 900 pounds, protecting workers from the dangers of moving heavy objects in tight spaces .

The automated system is integrated with a vertical CNC lathe that bores the center hole of railway wheels, ensuring a precise interference fit with the axle . The system includes measurement capabilities that enable precise boring, with access to pre- and post-machining measurement data enabling customers to analyze and extend the lives of wheels and maintenance machines . The system can be networked with other automated systems to create a closed-loop system, increasing productivity and decreasing the chance of operator error .

The Güdel system demonstrates the potential for automated material handling to transform manufacturing operations in the Tazewell County region. The region's manufacturing companies, including West River Conveyors, which produces heavy conveyor equipment, could benefit from similar automation. The integration of lowL-based control systems with automated material handling would enable companies to enhance safety, improve consistency, and increase productivity.

The Güdel system also demonstrates the importance of reliability and trust in automation components. Simmons chose the ZP-6 gantry system "primarily for its reliability and capability, because equipment availability is critical for our customers" . The company noted that "there is a level of trust and understanding of each other's capabilities that makes the projects run smoother" . This emphasis on reliability and trust is essential for the successful deployment of automation in manufacturing.

### The Electric Crane Revolution in Heavy Manufacturing

Sims Crane & Equipment Company's introduction of a battery-powered electric crane in the U.S. market demonstrates the potential for electrification to transform heavy manufacturing operations . The crane, an LR 1250.1 unplugged electric crawler crane from Liebherr, eliminates carbon emissions and is capable of operating for up to eight hours unplugged, or for an unlimited amount of time when connected to a power source .

The transition to electric equipment required significant changes in operations, including charging infrastructure and workforce training . Crane operators had to learn new skills, and the company cross-trained employees to handle both diesel and electric equipment . The company also focused on educating clients about the benefits of electric equipment, producing materials and explaining the advantages .

The electric crane revolution has direct applications to the manufacturing operations of companies in the Tazewell County region. The region's manufacturers, including those producing heavy equipment and components, could benefit from electrification of their material handling operations. The integration of electric equipment with lowL-based control systems would enable efficient energy management and performance optimization.

The Sims Crane example also demonstrates the importance of sustainability as a driver of innovation. The company's decision to introduce electric equipment was driven by both supply chain challenges and a commitment to sustainability . The electric crane provided a competitive advantage and a new potential revenue stream, while helping clients meet their sustainability goals .

### Technology Roadmap for the Region's Manufacturers

The integration of lowL-based control systems, robotics, and AI into the manufacturing operations of companies in the Tazewell County region should follow a phased approach, building on existing capabilities and investments. The technology roadmap should be tailored to the specific needs and capabilities of each company, recognizing that different companies will have different priorities and constraints.

**Phase 1: Foundation Building (0-12 Months)**

The first phase of the technology roadmap focuses on building the foundational capabilities needed for advanced automation. This includes training engineering staff in lowL programming, developing prototypes of control systems for specific applications, and piloting automation in limited areas. The lowL compiler and AlgorithmicOS provide the tools for this phase, enabling companies to develop real-time control systems for their specific applications [citation:compiler][citation:robotics_book].

During this phase, companies should identify specific applications where automation and lowL-based control systems can provide the greatest benefit. For Tate, this might be the automated cutting and forming of raised access floor panels. For VFP, this might be the robotic welding of enclosure components. For West River Conveyors, this might be the automated assembly of conveyor components. For White Rock Truss, this might be the automated cutting and assembly of truss components.

The foundation-building phase should also include workforce development, with training programs developed in partnership with VCEDA and local training providers. The VCEDA workforce development and training grants that have supported previous expansions can be leveraged for this purpose [citation:vfp]. The training programs should focus on the skills needed for lowL programming, robotics operation, and systems integration.

**Phase 2: Targeted Automation (12-24 Months)**

The second phase of the technology roadmap focuses on deploying targeted automation in specific applications, building on the prototypes developed in Phase 1. This phase includes the deployment of robotic systems for specific applications, the integration of lowL-based control systems with existing equipment, and the development of quality monitoring and control systems.

During this phase, companies should deploy robotic systems for applications that provide the greatest return on investment. For Tate, this might be the automated assembly of modular aisle containment systems. For VFP, this might be the automated metal forming and finishing of enclosure components. For West River Conveyors, this might be the automated machining of conveyor components. For White Rock Truss, this might be the automated inspection and packaging of trusses.

The targeted automation phase should also include the integration of the RoboBallet system for multi-robot coordination in complex assembly operations . The RoboBallet system's ability to coordinate up to eight robotic arms would enable companies to deploy teams of robots working together efficiently and safely. The system's AI-based planning would reduce the need for manual planning and enable rapid reconfiguration for different products.

**Phase 3: Full Integration (24-48 Months)**

The third phase of the technology roadmap focuses on the full integration of automation, AI, and lowL-based control systems across the manufacturing operation. This phase includes the deployment of comprehensive automation systems, the integration of AI for process optimization, and the development of closed-loop quality management systems.

During this phase, companies should deploy comprehensive automation systems that integrate all aspects of their manufacturing operations. This includes the integration of automated material handling, robotic assembly, quality monitoring, and process control. The Intrinsic workcell platform, with its software-first approach and AI capabilities, provides a model for this level of integration .

The full integration phase should also include the deployment of the Stanford assembly planning algorithm for optimizing multi-robot assembly . The algorithm's ability to generate optimal assembly plans quickly would enable companies to reconfigure their operations rapidly, responding to changes in product demand or design. The algorithm's support for parallelization would maximize throughput, increasing production capacity.

The full integration phase should also include the deployment of the LLM-powered low-code programming framework for enabling intuitive programming and control of robotic systems . The framework's ability to handle natural language instructions would enable operators to program and control robots without specialized programming skills, reducing the need for extensive training and enabling more widespread adoption of automation.

The technology integration would enable companies to increase production capacity, improve product quality, and reduce manufacturing costs, positioning them for continued growth in their respective markets.

The training programs should focus on the skills needed for lowL programming, robotics operation, and systems integration.

The productivity impact of technology integration is substantial, with the potential to increase output per worker significantly. The automation of manufacturing operations would enable companies to produce more products in less time, reducing lead times and improving customer satisfaction. The AI-based optimization would enable companies to make better use of their resources, reducing waste and improving efficiency.

The competitiveness impact of technology integration is significant, as companies that adopt advanced automation and AI will be better positioned to compete in global markets. The ability to produce higher quality products at lower costs, with greater flexibility and faster response times, would provide a competitive advantage that would be difficult for competitors to match.

The successful integration of lowL-based control systems, robotics, and AI into manufacturing operations requires a skilled workforce with expertise in programming, robotics operation, and systems integration. The manufacturing sector will be a pillar of the region's diversified economy, providing stable, well-paying jobs for workers in the region. The success of companies like Tate, VFP, West River Conveyors, and White Rock Truss demonstrates the potential for manufacturing to thrive in Southwest Virginia, even as the coal industry continues its long-term decline. The future of manufacturing in Tazewell County is bright, and its growth is central to the broader economic revitalization of the region.

# Part IV: The Unified Approach - US 19 Corridor Power Provider

## Chapter 10: Energy + Agriculture + Manufacturing Synergy

### Section 10.1: Integrated Site Planning

#### The Vision for a Unified Economic Landscape

The transition from a coal-dependent economy to a diversified, sustainable future for the US 19 corridor requires more than isolated projects in separate sectors; it demands a unified approach that integrates energy production, agriculture, manufacturing, and workforce development into a cohesive economic ecosystem. The concept of integrated site planning represents a fundamental shift in how the region approaches economic development, recognizing that the same landscapes that once supported coal extraction can now support renewable energy generation, sustainable forestry, advanced manufacturing, and agricultural production. This holistic approach maximizes the value of the region's land resources while creating synergies that enhance the economic impact of each individual project.

The unified approach builds on the region's existing assets: the extensive reclaimed mine lands that dot the landscape, the transportation infrastructure that was developed to support the coal industry, the skilled workforce that has been shaped by generations of industrial employment, and the institutional capacity that has been developed through organizations like the Virginia Coalfield Economic Development Authority. By integrating these assets into a comprehensive economic development strategy, the region can create a future that honors its industrial heritage while embracing the opportunities of the clean energy economy. The unified approach transforms liabilities into assets, turning abandoned mine sites into energy production facilities, degraded landscapes into productive forests, and displaced workers into the skilled labor force of the future.

The US 19 corridor serves as the spine of this unified approach, connecting the communities and projects that will define the region's economic future. The highway, which has been designated as a Corridor of Statewide Significance, provides the transportation link that enables the movement of goods, people, and resources essential to the region's economic development. The corridor studies being conducted by VDOT address safety and congestion concerns that have constrained the region's growth, while major infrastructure projects like the Beckley Z-Way improve access and connectivity. The rail legacy of the region, embodied by the CSX and Norfolk Southern networks, provides a ready-made logistics infrastructure that can support new industries, including the renewable energy sector, manufacturing, and agriculture.

#### Reclaimed Mine Lands as Multi-Purpose Assets

The thousands of acres of reclaimed mine lands that characterize the US 19 corridor represent one of the region's most significant assets, providing sites for energy production, forestry, and economic development that can be repurposed for multiple uses. The abandoned mine lands that were once environmental liabilities are now being recognized as valuable assets, with the potential to support a range of economic activities that contribute to the region's diversification. The Virginia Department of Energy, through the Abandoned Mine Land Economic Revitalization (AMLER) program, has been instrumental in transforming these sites into economic assets, with $11 million in federal funding available for projects that drive economic development in southwest Virginia's coalfield communities .

The AMLER program has successfully transformed former mining sites into vibrant economic assets across southwest Virginia since its inception in 2017 . Previous funding rounds have established industrial parks, renewable energy facilities, tourism destinations, and critical infrastructure improvements throughout the region . The U.S. Office of Surface Mining Reclamation and Enforcement selected Virginia as one of just six states to receive this competitive award, reflecting the Commonwealth's national leadership in innovative rural economic development . To qualify for funding, projects must be located on or adjacent to mine lands abandoned before 1977 and demonstrate strong economic development potential .

The Lewis Ridge Pumped Storage Project in Kentucky provides a compelling model for how reclaimed mine lands can be repurposed for renewable energy generation . The $1 billion project, which has submitted its final license application to the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission, will build a 266-megawatt pumped storage hydropower facility on reclaimed mine lands near Pineville, Kentucky . The project will create approximately 2,300 construction jobs and apprenticeships, with $104 million flowing into the local economy during the construction phase . Once operational, the facility will employ about 45 full-time workers and generate $16.5 million annually in local property tax revenue . The project is touted as both a tribute to the region's coal heritage and a model for sustainable development .

The Nature Conservancy's Mining the Sun initiative provides another model for repurposing reclaimed mine lands, with the organization working to convert previously mined sites into solar installations . The Cumberland Forest Project, which includes 253,000 acres of forest in far Southwest Virginia and Tennessee, includes the former Red Onion mine and other abandoned mining sites . The solar projects on these sites will generate new tax revenues, spending, temporary construction jobs, and permanent jobs in Central Appalachian communities that have experienced coal mine closures . The projects also take advantage of Inflation Reduction Act tax credits designed to incentivize renewable energy investments in "energy communities," providing an additional 10% tax credit for clean energy development built in these areas .

The Virginia legislature has acted to promote renewable energy development on brownfields, targeting the many former mine sites in Virginia's Southwest region . House Bill 1925 established the Virginia Brownfield and Coal Mine Renewable Energy Grant Fund, which awards grants on a competitive basis to renewable energy projects located on brownfields or previously mined lands . The grant program awards $500 per kilowatt for renewable energy projects located on previously coal mined lands, and $100 per kilowatt for projects located on brownfields, with a maximum award of $35 million per year . The legislation also required the Virginia Department of Energy to develop an online handbook for renewable energy development on brownfields and previously mined lands .

#### The Landscape-Scale Economic Transition

The concept of a "landscape-scale" economic transition recognizes that the economic transformation of the US 19 corridor must be comprehensive, addressing the full range of economic, social, and environmental challenges facing the region. The transition from coal to a diversified economy cannot be achieved through isolated projects in individual sectors; it requires a coordinated approach that integrates energy production, agriculture, manufacturing, and services into a cohesive economic ecosystem. The landscape-scale approach ensures that the benefits of the transition are distributed across the region, creating economic opportunities that support communities throughout the corridor.

The landscape-scale transition builds on the region's existing assets and infrastructure, recognizing that the same highways, rail lines, and utility corridors that supported the coal industry can now support the clean energy economy. The US 19 corridor, with its designation as a Corridor of Statewide Significance, provides the transportation backbone for the transition . The highway connects the communities of the corridor, from the agricultural valleys of Virginia to the industrial landscapes of West Virginia and Pennsylvania, enabling the movement of goods, people, and resources essential to economic development . The rail networks of CSX and Norfolk Southern provide a ready-made logistics infrastructure that can support new industries .

The landscape-scale transition also recognizes the importance of environmental restoration to the region's economic future. The reforestation of abandoned mine lands through programs like the Appalachian Regional Reforestation Initiative (ARRI) and the Forestry Reclamation Approach (FRA) provides both environmental and economic benefits. The restored forests sequester carbon, improve water quality, provide wildlife habitat, and create opportunities for timber production. The reforestation of mine lands also supports the region's agricultural heritage, with the restored forests providing grazing for livestock and timber for wood products. The environmental restoration of the region is thus not merely an ecological objective but an economic strategy, creating assets that support the region's diversification.

The landscape-scale transition is supported by federal and state programs that provide funding and technical assistance for economic development in coal-impacted communities. The AMLER program, which has provided $11 million in funding for the eighth round of projects in Virginia, is a cornerstone of the Commonwealth's rural revitalization strategy . The program transforms abandoned mine lands and surrounding areas into opportunities for economic growth, with previous funding rounds establishing industrial parks, renewable energy facilities, tourism destinations, and critical infrastructure improvements throughout the region . The program's focus on projects located on or adjacent to mine lands abandoned before 1977 ensures that the funding is targeted to the communities most affected by the coal industry's decline .

#### Brownfield Redevelopment: From Mining Sites to Energy Facilities

The redevelopment of brownfields, including abandoned mine lands, into renewable energy facilities represents one of the most promising opportunities for the US 19 corridor's economic transition. The brownfield redevelopment process transforms environmental liabilities into economic assets, creating value from sites that might otherwise remain blighted. The process of brownfield redevelopment is supported by federal and state programs that provide funding, technical assistance, and regulatory flexibility for projects that repurpose contaminated or degraded sites for productive use.

The EPA's Brownfield Grant Funding Program provides direct funding for brownfields assessment, cleanup, training, and research, with Virginia selected to receive $1.5 million in grant awards for brownfields assessment and cleanup funding in 2021 . The funding supports Northampton and Pittsylvania counties, the Southside Planning District Commission, and the City of Staunton to conduct assessment and cleanup activities . The EPA's RE-Powering America's Land Initiative encourages renewable energy development on brownfields, identifying the renewable energy potential of these sites and providing resources for communities and developers .

The Virginia Department of Environmental Quality has proposed guidance reclassifying brownfields and reclaimed coal fields as "redevelopment" rather than new development, which would reduce the stormwater management regulations' water quality improvement requirement . This regulatory flexibility makes brownfield redevelopment more economically viable, reducing the compliance costs that can constrain development. DEQ also has developed a Brownfields Dashboard showing potential brownfield redevelopment sites in Virginia, providing a resource for developers and communities interested in repurposing these sites .

The economic benefits of brownfield redevelopment are significant, with EPA data showing that land revitalization can increase residential property values near brownfield sites by 5% to over 15% when cleanup is completed . The redevelopment of brownfields also generates tax revenue, creates jobs, and stimulates economic activity that benefits the broader community. The brownfield redevelopment efforts often gain support from communities that are directly affected by the potentially contaminated lands' adverse environmental impacts or blight . The transformation of former mine sites into energy facilities thus provides both economic and environmental benefits, creating value from sites that might otherwise remain liabilities.

The redevelopment of brownfields for renewable energy production is supported by federal tax incentives designed to encourage investment in "energy communities," including brownfield sites . The Inflation Reduction Act provides a 10% tax credit for clean energy development built in energy communities, in addition to other tax credits for which clean energy projects may qualify . The tax credits reduce the cost of renewable energy development on brownfields, making these projects more economically viable and competitive with development on greenfield sites.

#### The Cumberland Forest Project: A Model for Integrated Land Use

The Nature Conservancy's Cumberland Forest Project provides a compelling model for integrated land use in the Appalachian region, demonstrating how former mine lands can be repurposed for renewable energy production while supporting conservation and community development. The project, which includes 253,000 acres of forest in far Southwest Virginia and Tennessee, includes the former Red Onion mine and other abandoned mining sites scattered across three counties in two states . The project seeks to demonstrate the potential for transforming former mine lands and brownfields into renewable energy hubs that integrate into wider economic diversification and conservation strategies .

The solar projects on the Cumberland Forest lands will generate new tax revenues, spending, temporary construction jobs, and a small number of permanent jobs in Central Appalachian communities that have experienced coal mine closures . Building on these important benefits, TNC and the solar developers have agreed to an additional community benefits plan that will engage local stakeholders to inform other investments that developers will make in community-identified priorities . The community engagement process is now underway with the Wildcats Solar project . The project is also soliciting competitive proposals for additional solar projects on former mine lands in partnership with LevelTen Energy, hoping to expand the possibilities for solar on the Tennessee and Kentucky portions of the property .

The Cumberland Forest Project takes advantage of the Inflation Reduction Act tax credits designed to incentivize renewable energy investments in "energy communities," including brownfield sites . The tax credits reduce the cost of renewable energy development on brownfields, making these projects more economically viable . The project has also been incorporated into a Virginia consortium application for the Department of Energy's new Clean Energy on Mine Lands grant program . The combination of federal incentives, state support, and private investment creates a financing structure that enables the transformation of former mine lands into productive assets.

The Cumberland Forest Project demonstrates the potential for integrated land use in the Appalachian region, combining renewable energy production, forest conservation, and community development in a single initiative. The project's approach of engaging local stakeholders in the development of community benefits plans ensures that the benefits of the project are distributed to the communities most affected by the coal industry's decline. The project's use of federal incentives and state programs reduces the cost of development, making the projects economically viable and competitive with development on greenfield sites.

#### Solar on Mine Lands: A Growing National Trend

The development of solar energy facilities on reclaimed mine lands is a growing national trend, with projects across the Appalachian region demonstrating the potential for former mining sites to support renewable energy production. The Martin County Solar Project in Kentucky, developed by Savion—a Shell Group portfolio company—is among the largest solar farms in the state and also one of the first U.S. solar projects built on a reclaimed coal mine site . The 111-MW facility entered commercial operation on reclaimed land at a former coal mine site, demonstrating the viability of solar development on degraded lands .

The Starfire Mine in Kentucky, which for years operated as one of the largest coal mines in Appalachia, is being transformed into a solar farm that will be the state's largest . The project, developed by a group that includes Rivian, the California-based electric vehicle manufacturer, will turn the site of the former coal mine into a renewable energy facility . The project demonstrates the potential for former coal mines to support the clean energy transition, providing land that is already disturbed and that has existing transmission infrastructure .

The solar projects on former mine lands benefit from the existing infrastructure that was developed to support the coal industry. Mines, brownfields, and former industrial sites are typically equipped with transmission lines, roads, and may have graded land and other infrastructure that renewable energy projects can leverage . This existing infrastructure reduces the cost of development, making solar projects on mine lands more economically viable . The projects also benefit from the land's already disturbed status, reducing the environmental impact of development and the need for additional land disturbance .

The solar projects on former mine lands provide economic benefits to the communities that have been affected by the coal industry's decline. The projects generate tax revenue, create jobs, and stimulate economic activity that benefits the broader community . The projects also demonstrate the potential for former mining communities to contribute to the nation's future energy needs in a transition to clean energy production . The projects thus provide both economic and symbolic benefits, demonstrating that the region can move beyond its coal heritage to embrace the clean energy economy.

#### Wind Energy Potential in the Appalachian Region

The wind energy potential of the Appalachian region is significant, with research showing a clear lack of wind turbines in Virginia and suggesting potential in Appalachia . Wind turbines can be placed in multi-use landscapes, fitting into different areas without greatly disrupting current land use . Annual average wind speed should be at least 9 mph for small wind turbines and 13 mph for utility-scale turbines . The Appalachian region fits these criteria, with the region's mountain ridges providing the wind resources needed for utility-scale wind development .

The development of wind energy on reclaimed mine lands in the Appalachian region would provide a new economic opportunity for communities affected by the coal industry's decline. The wind turbines can be placed on the ridgelines that were once the sites of coal mining operations, repurposing land that has already been disturbed. The wind projects would generate tax revenue, create jobs, and stimulate economic activity that benefits the broader community. The wind projects would also contribute to the clean energy transition, providing renewable energy that can power homes and businesses throughout the region.

The integration of wind energy with the region's other economic activities, including agriculture and forestry, would create synergies that enhance the economic impact of the projects. The wind turbines can be placed on multi-use landscapes, fitting into agricultural and forest lands without greatly disrupting current land use . The wind projects would provide a new income stream for landowners, supplementing agricultural and forestry income. The combination of wind energy with other economic activities would create a more resilient and diversified economic base for the region.

The development of wind energy in the Appalachian region is supported by federal incentives that encourage renewable energy investment in coal-impacted communities. The Inflation Reduction Act provides a 10% tax credit for clean energy development built in "energy communities," including brownfield sites . This incentive reduces the cost of wind development on mine lands, making these projects more economically viable. The combination of federal incentives, state support, and private investment creates a financing structure that enables the development of wind projects on reclaimed mine lands.

#### The Synergy of Agriculture, Energy, and Manufacturing

The integration of agriculture, energy, and manufacturing creates synergies that enhance the economic impact of each sector, with the outputs of one sector serving as inputs for another. The timber produced from reforested mine lands can be used for biomass energy production, supporting the region's energy independence while providing a market for the timber. The agricultural products of the region, including cattle, dairy, and crops, can be processed in local food manufacturing facilities, capturing more of the value chain and creating jobs. The renewable energy produced from solar, wind, and pumped storage hydropower can power the manufacturing facilities that process agricultural products and produce manufactured goods.

The synergy between agriculture and energy is particularly significant, with the region's agricultural production providing opportunities for renewable energy development. The manure from cattle operations can be used for biogas production, generating renewable energy while reducing the environmental impact of the operations. The agricultural lands can be used for solar development, with the solar panels providing shade for crops and livestock while generating electricity. The timber plantations on reclaimed mine lands can provide biomass for energy production, supporting the region's energy independence while providing a market for the timber.

The synergy between energy and manufacturing is also significant, with the renewable energy produced in the region providing the power for manufacturing operations. The manufacturing facilities that produce equipment for renewable energy projects, including the components for solar arrays, wind turbines, and pumped storage hydropower facilities, create jobs and economic activity that support the region's energy transition. The manufacturing facilities also provide a market for the region's energy production, creating a closed-loop system that supports both sectors.

The synergy between agriculture and manufacturing is equally important, with the agricultural products of the region providing the raw materials for food manufacturing. The cattle and dairy products produced in the region can be processed in local facilities, creating value-added products that can be sold in regional and national markets. The timber produced from reforested mine lands can be processed in local sawmills, creating wood products that can be used in construction and manufacturing. The agricultural and forestry products of the region thus support a manufacturing sector that creates jobs and economic activity.

#### Workforce Training Programs

The success of the integrated development strategy depends on the availability of a skilled workforce, and the region has made significant investments in workforce development and training. The Virginia Coalfield Economic Development Authority has provided substantial funding for workforce development, including a $200,000 grant to the Mountain Empire Community College (MECC) Foundation for workforce development and training . The funds will be used to support future training and certification needs for residents in the VCEDA service territory for the next two years, meeting the ongoing demand for skilled, trained workers in high-need manufacturing, technology, and health care sectors .

The VCEDA workforce training grant has served as a lifeline for Mountain Empire Community College students who are obtaining nationally recognized credentials and professional licensures in their fields . Often, the costs for these certifications are a financial barrier for students, and the grant funding removes that barrier . MECC anticipates training 500 or more students in 2025-2026, with existing industries expanding their employment offerings . In total, more than $1.2 million in grants have been approved by VCEDA to the MECC Foundation since an initial $500,000 grant approved in 2018 . Since the initial grant, approximately 1,313 students have completed 1,426 certifications and training courses to meet workforce needs in the region .

The Southwest Virginia Community College (SWCC) Educational Foundation has also received substantial support, with a $404,930 grant from VCEDA closed recently . The funds will be used for workforce development and training, for the purchase of orbital welding units and to support the Small Business Development Center at SWCC . The purchase of two orbital welding units for training purposes will support training in high purity piping systems for the food and beverage industry . These systems require precise, contamination-free welds to meet strict sanitary and regulatory standards . In addition, precision welding technology is widely used in industries including aerospace, energy, pharmaceuticals, and advanced manufacturing .

The workforce development programs supported by VCEDA are essential to the region's economic development, providing the skilled workers needed for manufacturing, energy, and technology jobs. The grant funding has supported training for a range of employers, including Scott County Telephone Cooperative, VFP, Komatsu, Ballad Health, and Pike Electric . These employers have created hundreds of jobs in the Southwest Virginia region, building long-term, sustainable employment and a renewed economy for the coalfield region . The workforce development programs also support the Small Business Development Center in its assistance to entrepreneurs and small business owners in the VCEDA region .

#### Conclusion: A Unified Vision for the Future

The unified approach to economic development in the US 19 corridor integrates energy production, agriculture, manufacturing, and workforce development into a cohesive economic ecosystem that builds on the region's existing assets and infrastructure. The reclaimed mine lands that dot the landscape are being repurposed for renewable energy production, sustainable forestry, and economic development, transforming environmental liabilities into economic assets. The transportation infrastructure that was developed to support the coal industry is being repurposed to support the clean energy economy, with the US 19 corridor serving as the spine of the region's economic development.

The landscape-scale economic transition of the region is supported by federal and state programs that provide funding and technical assistance for economic development in coal-impacted communities. The AMLER program, the EPA's Brownfield Grant Funding Program, and the Inflation Reduction Act tax credits provide the financial support that enables the transformation of former mine sites into renewable energy facilities. The workforce development programs supported by VCEDA provide the skilled workers needed for the region's new industries, ensuring that the benefits of the economic transition are realized by the region's residents.

The synergy between agriculture, energy, and manufacturing creates opportunities for value creation that go beyond what any single sector could achieve. The timber produced from reforested mine lands supports biomass energy production, while the agricultural products of the region support food manufacturing. The renewable energy produced in the region powers the manufacturing facilities, creating a closed-loop system that supports both sectors. The integrated approach maximizes the value of the region's land resources while creating synergies that enhance the economic impact of each individual project.

The US 19 corridor serves as the backbone of this unified approach, connecting the communities and projects that will define the region's economic future. The highway, with its designation as a Corridor of Statewide Significance, provides the transportation link that enables the movement of goods, people, and resources essential to the region's economic development. The rail networks of CSX and Norfolk Southern provide a ready-made logistics infrastructure that can support new industries, while the corridor studies being conducted by VDOT address safety and congestion concerns that have constrained the region's growth.

The unified approach to economic development in the US 19 corridor provides a model for other coal-impacted communities seeking to transition to a diversified economic future. The approach honors the region's industrial heritage while embracing the opportunities of the clean energy economy, creating a future that is both sustainable and prosperous. The success of the approach depends on continued collaboration between the public and private sectors, with government providing the financing and infrastructure that enable private companies to invest and grow. The future of the US 19 corridor is bright, and the unified approach to economic development is central to that bright future.
---

### Part V: Project Implementation and Resource Guide

**Chapter 11: Implementation Roadmap**

# Chapter Eleven: Implementation Roadmap

## Section 11.1: Near-Term Actions (1-2 Years) — A Comprehensive Implementation Guide

### The Foundation of Transformation: A Two-Year Action Plan

The transformation of the US 19 corridor from a coal-dependent economy to a diversified, sustainable economic ecosystem requires a clear and actionable roadmap that guides investments, builds partnerships, and creates momentum for long-term change. The near-term actions outlined in this section represent the foundational steps that must be taken within the first 24 months to position the region for success. These actions are designed to be achievable, measurable, and impactful, creating the conditions for the larger investments and transformations that will follow in the medium and long term.

The two-year action plan is built on four interconnected pillars: completing feasibility studies for critical energy infrastructure, securing federal grants that provide the capital for large-scale investments, developing entrepreneurial ecosystems that foster innovation and job creation, and expanding broadband and cellular connectivity that enables participation in the digital economy. Each of these pillars is essential to the region's success, and each requires coordinated action by government, business, and community leaders. The actions are designed to build on each other, with the feasibility studies providing the foundation for grant applications, the grants providing the capital for infrastructure investments, the entrepreneurial ecosystem creating the businesses that will utilize the infrastructure, and the connectivity enabling all of these activities to thrive.

The implementation of the near-term actions will require collaboration among a diverse set of stakeholders, including federal and state agencies, regional economic development organizations, local governments, businesses, educational institutions, and community organizations. The Virginia Coalfield Economic Development Authority (VCEDA) will play a central role in coordinating these efforts, leveraging its relationships with state and federal agencies and its deep understanding of the region's needs and opportunities. The Appalachian Regional Commission (ARC) will provide critical funding and technical assistance through its POWER Initiative, which has already invested $484.7 million in 564 projects impacting coal-impacted counties since 2015 . The success of the near-term actions will depend on the active engagement of all of these stakeholders, working together toward a shared vision of economic transformation.

For a plan implementor, the first step is to establish a centralized project management office (PMO) that can coordinate the various workstreams, track progress against milestones, and serve as the primary point of contact for federal and state agencies. This PMO should be staffed with individuals who have experience in grant management, project finance, and community engagement, and should be empowered to make decisions and allocate resources across the various initiatives. The PMO should also establish regular reporting mechanisms to ensure that all stakeholders are informed of progress and that any issues are identified and addressed promptly.

---

### Completing Feasibility Studies for Pumped Storage Hydropower Sites

The completion of feasibility studies for pumped storage hydropower (PSH) sites in the US 19 corridor is the most critical near-term action for the region's energy future, providing the technical foundation for the development of large-scale energy storage that can support the transition to renewable energy. Dominion Energy began evaluating the potential for a pumped hydroelectric storage power station in Tazewell County following the passage of 2017 legislation encouraging the development of pumped storage technology in Southwest Virginia. The legislation, sponsored by Sen. Ben Chafin, Del. Terry Kilgore, and Del. Todd Pillion, was signed into law by Gov. Terry McAuliffe and requires that at least part of the energy used to operate the facility be generated by renewable resources in the area.

The Tazewell County site, located on East River Mountain, has been the primary focus of Dominion Energy's feasibility studies since the company filed a preliminary permit application with the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission (FERC) in September 2017. The site encompasses approximately 2,600 acres owned by Dominion Energy and offers an elevation drop of approximately 1,000 feet, which is ideal for pumped storage operations. The facility would function like a giant battery, storing energy when demand is low and quickly delivering power to the grid when demand is high, while also helping to balance the fluctuating nature of renewable energy resources.

The feasibility studies for the Tazewell County site have been extensive, including environmental, geological, archeological, and technical assessments. In 2018, Dominion Energy conducted extensive core drilling at eight different sites and constructed a road on the top of the mountain to access the upper reservoir site. The company hosted informational open house events in Tazewell and Bland Counties in July 2019 and planned to file a pre-application document with FERC in late 2019. However, the project has experienced delays, with the COVID-19 pandemic slowing progress in 2020. As of 2022, Dominion Energy confirmed it was still evaluating the feasibility of the project.

The Bullitt Mine site in Wise County, which was initially considered as an alternative to the Tazewell County site, has been determined to be unsuitable for a utility-scale pumped storage project. The decision was based on an analysis of the site by the Virginia Center for Coal & Energy Research, which indicated that the abandoned mine cavities could not support the requirements of a large-scale PSH facility. The Bullitt Mine, which has been closed since 1997 and is currently flooded, had been considered because its underground cavities could theoretically be used as a lower reservoir. However, the feasibility study determined that the site is not suitable for the scale of project that would be economically viable.

**Steps for a Plan Implementor to Complete PSH Feasibility Studies**

1. **Engage with Dominion Energy and FERC**: The implementor should establish a formal partnership or working group with Dominion Energy to understand the current status of their feasibility studies and to identify any gaps that need to be addressed. This may involve reviewing existing data, conducting additional site assessments, and coordinating with FERC on the licensing process. The implementor should also engage with the Virginia Department of Energy to ensure state-level support for the project.

2. **Secure Funding for Supplementary Studies**: While Dominion Energy has funded the initial feasibility work, additional studies may be needed to satisfy federal grant requirements or to address specific concerns raised by stakeholders. The implementor should identify potential funding sources for supplementary studies, including the ARC POWER Initiative, which offers up to $100,000 for planning awards, and the DOE Office of Clean Energy Demonstrations (OCED), which provides funding for clean energy demonstration projects . The implementor should prepare concept papers for these funding opportunities, ensuring that they address all the requirements described in the FOA, including the Demonstration Plan, Community Benefits Plan, and Management and Organization sections .

3. **Coordinate with Federal and State Agencies**: The implementor should establish regular communication with FERC, the U.S. Army Corps of Engineers, the Environmental Protection Agency, and the Virginia Department of Environmental Quality to understand the permitting requirements and timelines. This coordination should begin early in the feasibility process to identify potential issues and to develop mitigation strategies. The implementor should also work with the Virginia Department of Energy to ensure that the project aligns with the state's energy planning goals and to secure any necessary state permits.

4. **Develop a Community Benefits Plan**: As part of the feasibility process, the implementor should develop a comprehensive Community Benefits Plan that outlines the economic, social, and environmental benefits of the project for the region. This plan should include details on job creation, tax revenue, workforce development, and community investments, and should be developed in consultation with local stakeholders. The Community Benefits Plan is a requirement for many federal grant applications, including those submitted to OCED , and will be essential for securing public support for the project.

5. **Prepare for FERC Licensing**: The implementor should work with Dominion Energy to prepare for the FERC licensing process, which will require a detailed license application that addresses the environmental, technical, and economic aspects of the project. The implementor should ensure that all necessary studies are completed and that the application is submitted in a timely manner. The implementor should also prepare for the public consultation process that is required by FERC, engaging with local communities and stakeholders to address their concerns and to build support for the project.

The completion of the Tazewell County feasibility studies is essential for the project to advance to the next stage of development. The studies will determine the technical viability of the project, the environmental impacts that must be mitigated, and the economic benefits that the project would generate. According to a study by Chmura Economics & Analytics, the project would generate significant economic benefits for both Southwest Virginia and the entire Commonwealth, with the 10-year construction window potentially bringing more than 2,000 jobs to Southwest Virginia. The study also estimated millions in tax revenue to the counties involved, with Tazewell County projected to receive approximately $2.5 million annually.

The next steps for the Tazewell County PSH project include the completion of the remaining feasibility studies, the filing of a license application with FERC, and the securing of financing for construction. The project is estimated to cost approximately $2 billion. If the project moves forward, it would represent one of the largest economic development investments in the history of the region, creating thousands of jobs during construction and generating tax revenue that would support local governments for decades. The project would also contribute to Virginia's clean energy goals, providing large-scale energy storage that can support the integration of renewable energy sources.

---

### Securing Federal Grants: DOE Office of Clean Energy Demonstrations

The Department of Energy's Office of Clean Energy Demonstrations (OCED) represents one of the most significant sources of federal funding for large-scale clean energy projects in the US 19 corridor, with the Appalachian Regional Clean Hydrogen Hub (ARCH2) serving as a model for the scale of investment that can be secured. OCED has awarded up to $925 million for the development of the ARCH2 hydrogen hub, which will produce clean hydrogen to decarbonize hard-to-abate industries such as manufacturing and transportation. The hub has already received $30 million for Phase 1, which is expected to last up to 36 months. The remaining funds will be released in future phases as ARCH2 achieves determined milestones set by the DOE.

The ARCH2 hub is designed to use the region's natural gas resources, plus biomass and clean electricity, to produce hydrogen at sites located throughout West Virginia, Pennsylvania, and Ohio, while simultaneously capturing and permanently storing CO2 emissions deep within geologic formations. The hub plans to use the hydrogen produced to aid in the decarbonization of the industrial and transportation sectors. The economic benefits of the hub are substantial, including thousands of new jobs and training for local energy workers, with 40% of overall program benefits allocated to communities impacted by underinvestment and pollution.

OCED also provides funding for distributed energy systems (DES) demonstrations, with up to $50 million awarded for projects that implement distributed energy resource management systems (DERMS) . One such project, the GRID-FLEXER project led by Dominion Energy Virginia, plans to demonstrate a DERMS in Virginia's southeastern region, coordinating 150 MW of clean energy from distributed resources to provide real-time grid support and capacity . This model could be replicated in the US 19 corridor for the Tazewell County PSH project, which would similarly provide grid support and capacity by storing and releasing energy as needed.

The ARCH2 model and the GRID-FLEXER project demonstrate the potential for the region to secure significant federal funding for clean energy projects. The DOE OCED's Regional Hydrogen Hubs program has demonstrated the agency's willingness to invest in coal-impacted communities, providing a template for other clean energy investments in the region. The success of ARCH2 in securing Phase 1 funding demonstrates the viability of the hub model and the potential for similar investments in the US 19 corridor.

**Steps for a Plan Implementor to Secure DOE OCED Grants**

1. **Identify Relevant Funding Opportunities**: The implementor should monitor the OCED website and the Federal Register for notices of funding opportunities (NOFOs) that are relevant to the region's clean energy projects. This includes opportunities for pumped storage hydropower, renewable energy generation, energy storage, and advanced manufacturing. The implementor should also review the OCED Frequently Asked Questions document to understand the eligibility requirements and application process for specific programs .

2. **Submit a Concept Paper**: The OCED application process requires a two-step submission: a Concept Paper followed by a full Application. The Concept Paper must include a Cover Page, Demonstration Plan & Project Team Description, Community Benefits Plan, and Management and Organization sections . The implementor should prepare a compelling Concept Paper that clearly articulates the project's goals, the team's qualifications, the community benefits, and the management structure. The Concept Paper must be limited to a single project site or an aggregated set of proposed project sites .

3. **Establish Eligibility for Mine Land Funding**: If the project is located on mine land, the implementor must demonstrate that the site is eligible under the Surface Mining Control and Reclamation Act of 1977 (SMCRA) or the Mining Law of 1872 . This requires providing copies of Title V permits from the applicable permitting authority or demonstrating that the application site is in the Title IV database maintained by the Office of Surface Mining Reclamation and Enforcement . For Mining Law of 1872 claims, the implementor must provide a title search or claims filed with the appropriate body . The implementor should work with legal counsel to ensure that all eligibility documentation is complete and accurate.

4. **Engage with OCED Technical Assistance**: OCED provides technical assistance to applicants through its eXCHANGE portal and through partnerships with national laboratories like NREL . The implementor should leverage these resources to strengthen the application and to address any technical questions that arise.
